...how to make the **Mercury of Life** <note>original: *Mercurius vitae*; a potent medicinal white powder made from antimony, also known as Powder of Algaroth</note> is well known. However, I consider it necessary to remind the reader of this: if the **Mercury of Life** is not prepared correctly, it will more rightly be a **Mercury of Death** <note>original: *Mercurius mortis*</note> than a mercury of life. 

Take half a pound of **Martial Regulus** <note>antimony that has been purified or "reduced" using iron (Mars)</note> with <term>antimony</term> <note>original: *♁io / antimonio*</note>, and one full pound of the best **rectified mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii optimè rectificati*; mercury that has been purified through distillation</note>. Rub each **separately** <note>original: *à part*</note> into a powder, then mix the powders together. Place the mixture into a **retort** <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> with a wide neck. Let it stand in a cellar for several days and nights, unsealed. Afterward, place the retort in a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original: *Sand-Capelle*; a container filled with sand used to distribute heat evenly and prevent the glass from cracking</note> and apply the appropriate fire. A beautiful white <term>butter</term> <note>original: *Butyrum*; specifically "Butter of Antimony" (antimony trichloride), which has a thick, wax-like consistency</note> will pass over, and the mercury will be **revived** <note>returned to its metallic state</note>. 

Let the butter melt over a gentle fire, pour it into another suitable retort, and **rectify** <note>purify by redistilling</note> it; do this at least five times. Through such distillation, the butter is considerably altered, so that the **Mercury of Life** precipitated from it does not cause **vomiting** <note>original: *Vomitus*</note>, but only acts as a **laxative** <note>original: *laxiret*</note>. 

Precipitate it in this way: Pour distilled water—and a great deal of it—over the butter, and a delicate white powder will fall. This should be most diligently <term>cleansed</term> <note>original: *edulcoriren*; the process of washing a solid substance with water to remove any remaining acids or salts</note> with distilled water. Finally, dry the powder, place it in a flask, and pour **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritum Vini*; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note>...

...process a little, and thus set it up:

## 1. Preparation of Sulphur from
->Antimony.<-

In the name of God, take the **Sulphur of Antimony** <note>original: *Sulphur Antimonii*; in the Paracelsian tradition, this is the "soul" or combustible principle of the mineral</note>, as it was prepared with the **Circulat** <note>original: *Circulat*; likely the *Circulatum Minus*, a potent alchemical solvent or "circulated" spirit used for extractions</note>. Distill this Sulphur with the Circulat several times; afterwards, separate the Circulat from it by means of the **most highly rectified Spirit of Wine** <note>original: *Spiritu Vini rectificatissimo*; high-proof alcohol used to wash or purify substances</note>, so that the Sulphur remains behind as a thick, deep red **liquid** <note>original: *Liquor*</note>, without any **corrosive** <note>original: *Corrosiv*; a harsh chemical agent that eats away at matter</note>.

## 2. Preparation of Mercury.

This has been sufficiently taught: therefore, take it now and distill the Circulat from it, so that the **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*; the "spirit" or volatile principle</note> remains behind "dead," as a red **precipitate** <note>original: *Præcipitat*; a solid substance that has settled out of a solution</note>. For take note! The Circulat dissolves all **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> and makes them volatile, but it **coagulates** <note>thickens or solidifies</note> the Mercury. <term>Basilius</term> <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary and likely pseudonymous 15th-century alchemist known for his mastery of antimony</note> commands the use of **Oil of Vitriol** <note>original: *Oleum Victrioli*; sulfuric acid</note> for such work; but it must without fail have been no common sort. For the common kind indeed **calcinates** <note>original: *calciniret*; the process of reducing a substance to powder through heat or chemical reaction</note> the Mercury when it is drawn off, but only into a white powder; this one, however, is supposed to be red. ## **Mercury** or **Quicksilver** <note>original: *Mercurius oder Queck-Silber*</note>

*In Mercury is whatever they seek wisely* <note>original: *Est in Mercurio quidquid quærunt sapienter*</note>, is an ancient rule of the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the long lineage of alchemists who sought the Philosopher's Stone</note>: In **Mercury** is what the wise seek. This **Subject** <note>original: *Subjectum*; here meaning the underlying substance or matter being studied</note> is in truth a **Subject of all wonders** <note>original: *Subjectum omnis mirabilitatis*</note>. It is water and yet does not make things wet; earth, and yet is **volatile** <note>able to be vaporized</note>; a spirit, and yet, next to gold, the heaviest creature in the whole **sublunary creation** <note>original: *sublunarischen*; referring to the earthly realm below the orbit of the moon, subject to change and decay</note>; a body, and yet it penetrates everything. In short, **Mercury** is a **homogeneous entity** <note>original: *Ens homogeneum*; a substance that is uniform in its essence</note>, like gold; and that water from which all **metals** have their origin and essence. With good reason and right, one can call it an **unripe gold**, which through mere **digestion** <note>original: *Digestion*; the alchemical process of maturation through gentle, steady heat</note> can be brought to the ripeness of gold.

The works which the **Alchemists** have undertaken with this, their **mock-bird** <note>original: *Spott-Vogel*; a nickname for Mercury because it "mocks" the alchemist by easily escaping as vapor or changing form</note>, are countless: But because all preparations...

Every conscientious **physician** <note>original: *Medicus*</note> as well as every diligent alchemist should guard themselves against such prepared Mercury, for it brings nothing but harm, no matter how much certain physicians and **surgeons** <note>original: *Chyrurgi*</note> flatter themselves with a certain red and green **precipitate** <note>These colorful mercury oxides and chlorides were commonly used in early modern medicine but were often highly toxic</note>, and thereby deceive themselves and others. ### *The Preparation of Cinnabar* <note>original: *Cinnabaris Confectio*</note>

Take 2 parts **Quicksilver**, 1 part common **sulfur**, let the sulfur melt in a bowl, stir the Quicksilver quickly into it until none of it is visible anymore, and thus you will obtain a black mass <note>chemically known as *Aethiops mineralis*</note>; grind this small, and mix in half its weight of common **table salt**, place it in a **sublimation pot** <term>sublimation pot: a vessel used to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then collects as a solid again at the top</term>, set it in hot sand, and so the **Mercury** rises above the salt with as much sulfur as it needs for its **coagulation**, and leaves the impurities with the

### Antimonial Cinnabar <note>original: *Cinnabaris Antimonii*</note> is made in this way.

Take 2 lbs of **Antimony**, crush it small, and mix into it 4 lbs of **Sublimated Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii Sublimati*; mercury(II) chloride, a highly toxic white crystalline salt</note>. Place the mixture into a **retort** <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</term> with a wide neck. Distill it in a **sand bath** <term>sand bath: a container filled with sand that is heated to provide a uniform temperature to the glass vessel placed inside it</term>; first, use a gentle fire, and the **Butter** <note>original: *Butyrum*; here referring to "Butter of Antimony" (antimony trichloride), which has an oily, butter-like consistency</note> will pass over. Afterward, using a strong fire, the **cinnabar** <note>a bright red mineral consisting of mercury sulfide</note> sublimes.

I am accustomed to sublimating the cinnabar in this manner: as soon as the **Butter** has come over, I place the retort—which must be coated on the bottom <note>likely referring to "lorication," or coating the glass with clay to protect it from direct flame</note>—into a glowing bed of coals. In this way, the cinnabar sublimes quickly, and one can observe quite clearly through the glass when the **sublimation** <term>sublimation: the chemical process where a solid turns into a vapor when heated and then reverts to a solid upon cooling, used here to purify the cinnabar</term> is finished. For according to the common method, the cinnabar rises with difficulty, and one cannot know when enough is enough; instead, one must work like a blind man. In that case, if one works longer than necessary, the cinnabar is ruined; however, if one stops too early, one receives little, some-

### Natural Cinnabar <note>original: *Cinnabaris nativa*</note>

distilled together, I found a **trace of gold** <note>original: *Gold-Spuhr*</note>. It was indeed a wonder that this black powder, which was **quicksilver**, remained behind in such a fire in which the **cinnabar** sublimed. I am of the opinion that this powder would have become **fixed** <term>fixed: in alchemy, a substance that has been rendered solid and resistant to evaporation or change by fire</term> in a short amount of time. This mercury, as well as that from the cinnabar, is pure and more suitable for the following works than common mercury. When the cinnabar is ground, it is exceedingly beautiful and magnificent for use in **medicine**.

## Sublimated Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius Sublimatus*</note>

Because this is frequently used for various works, its process is this: Dissolve the **quicksilver** in **strong water** <term>Aqua fortis: nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to dissolve metals</term>; into the **solution**, add little by little twice as much common **salt** as there was quicksilver. Distill everything that will come over from a **glass flask** <note>original: *Kolben Glas*</note> and capture it in a **receiver** <note>original: *Recipient*</note>. At the end, under a strong fire, red **spirits** <note>acidic vapors</note> will pass over, and this **liquid** <note>original: *Liquor*</note> is as good as **royal water** <term>Aqua Regis: "King's Water," a highly corrosive mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</term>. At the very last, increase the fire even more, so that the **mercury** sublimes above the salt: the subtle **flowers** <term>flowers: the fine, powdery crystals that form at the top of a vessel during sublimation</term>

# Now follow the actual and philosophical preparations and fixations of Mercury.

## 1. Coagulation of Mercury by the force of its own internal Sulfur. <note>original: *Coagulatio Mercurii vi interni sui Sulphuris*</note>

**Animate** the **mercury** from **cinnabar** with the **martial regulus of antimony** <term>martial regulus of antimony: a metallic form of antimony produced by smelting it with iron (associated with Mars)</term>, as is taught in detail in the **Chapter on the Sun** <note>The "Sun" or *Sol* is the alchemical code for gold.</note>. Take <note>original: *℞*, the symbol for *recipe*</note> afterwards as much of that **mercury** as you wish, put it into a convenient little **retort**, and distill the **mercury** over from the **sand** <term>sand bath: a container of sand heated from below to provide a steady, even temperature for glass vessels</term> quite slowly in a gentle fire. A **red powder** will remain at the bottom, but not much at one time; if one provides such a strong fire that the sand glows, the powder melts in part and becomes **pure gold**. But this is not necessary; rather, one should pour the **mercury**—which is stuck in the neck [of the flask] or has also passed over—back over the powder...

### 2. The Fixation of Mercury.

Theophrastus <note>Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim, known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals in medicine.</note> writes of this, as does Helmontius <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and early chemistry.</note>, and indeed Theophrastus says: *Precipitate* the mercury with a **strong water** <term>Aqua fort: nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to dissolve metals</term> into a red powder; then draw off from it a graded **strong water**, and wash the **precipitate** <note>the solid substance that settles at the bottom of a solution after a chemical reaction</note> with **spirit of wine** <term>Spiritu Vini: high-proof distilled alcohol</term>. Thus you have **Diaphoretic Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium Diaphoreticum*; a medicinal preparation intended to induce sweating to purge the body of toxins</note>, a magnificent medicine **for all wounds, ulcers, the French disease** <note>original: *Morbo Gallico*; a historical name for syphilis</note>, etc. Furthermore, it brings joy to every ruined alchemist <note>likely referring to those who have spent their fortunes seeking the Stone without success</note>, as it is an increase of gold and can be melted with gold into good gold. Helmontius explains it thus: **Take the powder of Johannes de Vigo, etc.** <note>original: *Accipe pulverem Johannis de Vigo*; Johannes de Vigo was a famous 15th-century surgeon known for his medicinal "red powder" of mercury.</note> That is: Take a mercury that has been **precipitated** with **strong water**, and which you...

**Mercurius Diaphoreticus** <term>Diaphoretic Mercury: a medicinal preparation of mercury intended to induce sweating and expel toxins from the body</term>, a universal medicine for all internal and external ailments. Its dose is from 1 to 4 grains. Whoever possesses a pound of this has enough for the rest of his life. For he can set aside half as medicine, while augmenting the other half in the following manner:

**Recipe** <note>original: *Recipe*; take</note> 2 ounces of **fixed precipitate** <note>original: *Præcipitati fixi*; a chemical solid that has been rendered resistant to evaporation by heat</note>, or **Mercurius Diaphoreticus**; heat it gently until it glows under a **muffle** <term>muffle: a clay chamber or oven that protects the material from direct flame and ashes</term>. Put this into a **vial**, place it in a **sand bath** <term>sand-capelle: a container filled with sand and heated from below to provide a steady, even temperature</term>, and apply **fire by degrees** <note>original: *Feuer per gradus*; a technique of gradually increasing heat over time</note> for four weeks. 

In the beginning, let the heat be gentle; after the first week, make it increasingly stronger until the sand glows in the fourth week. In this way, the mercury that was added also becomes **fixed** <note>non-volatile; resistant to heat</note>, like fine gold. 

If 8 ounces of the **Mercurius Diaphoreticus** are used at once, one will have **fixed** 2 ounces of **prepared mercury** alongside it in four weeks, which one can then melt and turn into gold to provide for one's **sustenance** <note>original: *Zehrung*; livelihood or travel-money</note>. ## Of Vitriol or Copper-Water.

**Vitriol** <note>original: *Victriolum*</note>, or **copper-water**, is a salty **mineral liquid** <term>mineral liquid: literally "mountain-juice" (*Berg-Safft*), a term used for the acidic, mineral-rich water found naturally in mines</term> which, as it passes through the metallic veins in the mountains, corrodes some parts of the metals or ores and, according to the nature of those metals, takes on a particular property. Therefore, there are different types of vitriol, such as **copper vitriol** <note>original: *Victriolum Veneris*; blue vitriol</note>, **iron vitriol** <note>original: *Victriolum Martis*; green vitriol</note>, vitriol mixed from both iron and copper, **solar vitriol** <term>solar vitriol: *Victriolum Solare*, a high-quality vitriol associated with gold, such as the variety found in Salzburg</term>, and **white vitriol** <note>original: *Victriolum album*</note>, such as the Roman variety, which contains a white metallic earth, etc. The most common are copper and iron vitriol; however, they all carry a burning sulfur with them. The **preparations** <note>original: *Præparata*</note> made from each and every one are also many and varied: We will

...however, we will only note here the most necessary and important [preparations] for **chemistry** <note>original: *Chymie*; in this period, the term encompassed both practical chemical industry and alchemical pursuit</note>.

### Spirit & Oil of Vitriol.

**Take** <note>original: *℞*, the traditional apothecary symbol for *Recipe*</note> **vitriol**, boil it in a pot until the wild moisture has evaporated and the **vitriol** has become a hard stone. Crush this stone into powder and mix in twice as much <note>original: *noch einmahl so viel*; meaning an equal amount of flints to the amount of vitriol</note> **calcined** <term>calcined: a material heated to high temperatures to remove volatile components, making it dry and crumbly</term> finely crushed **flints** <note>original: *Feuer-Steine*; these were added to the vitriol to prevent it from melting into a solid mass, allowing the acidic vapors to escape more easily</note>. Put the mixture into a **coated retort** <term>coated retort: a glass distillation vessel reinforced with a "lutum" or coating of clay and dung to protect it from the direct heat of the furnace</term>, wall the **retort** into a suitable furnace, **lute** <term>lute: to seal the connection between the retort and the receiver with a specialized paste to prevent the escape of vapors</term> a large **receiver** <note>original: *Recipienten*</note> to it, and let everything dry well. 

Then, build a gentle fire beneath it (Note: one may place a little water in the **receiver** beforehand) and distill; first, a **phlegm** <term>phlegm: the watery, least potent part of a distillate</term> comes over, thereafter the **spirit**, and finally, with a strong fire, the so-called red **oil** <note>original: *Oleum*; this "oil of vitriol" is what we now identify as concentrated sulfuric acid</note>. When it has cooled, take everything that has distilled over from the **receiver** and put it into a glass flask. First, distill the **phlegm** from it and save it: it is a good cooling agent for **burning fevers**. After the **phlegm**, when the **spirits** begin to come over sour, lute the **receiver** on and distill the **spirit** over as well. When the process slows, let the fire go out; in the flask there remains

...then the red **Oil of Vitriol** <note>original: *Oleum Victrioli*; a concentrated form of sulfuric acid</note> is left behind. This is a very well-known method of distilling **vitriol**, except that in the common way, **calcined flints** <note>flints heated until they are brittle and white</note> are not used for this purpose.

From the **dead head** <note>original: *Capite mortuo*; the dry, solid residue remaining in the retort after all liquids and spirits have been driven off by heat</note>, which is called **colcothar** <note>the brownish-red iron oxide residue</note>, one can extract a salt which is useful for the **cementation** <term>cementation: a process where a metal is surrounded by powdered materials and heated to purify or harden it</term> of silver, in order to make it compact. **Helmont** <note>Jean Baptiste van Helmont (1580–1644), a famous Flemish chemist</note> and **Boyle** <note>Robert Boyle (1627–1691), a founding figure of modern chemistry</note> mix the **colcothar** with **sal ammoniac** <term>of each</term> <gloss>equal parts</gloss> and sublimate from it the **first essence of Venus** <note>original: *primum ens Veneris*; a medicinal compound of copper and iron believed to have great healing powers</note>, from which **Butler’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Butleri*; a legendary medicinal preparation named after the Irish physician William Butler</note> is made using **sea salt** <note>original: *Sale marino*</note> and **skull moss** <term>Usnea Cranii humani: a lichen that grows on human skulls, highly valued in the 17th century for treating ailments of the head</term>.

**Basilius Valentinus** <note>a legendary alchemical author, supposed to be a 15th-century monk</note> and his successor **Jodocus von Rehe** make a **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapidem Philosophorum*; the ultimate goal of alchemy, capable of transmuting metals and prolonging life</note> from it as follows:

Take **Hungarian vitriol**, dissolve it in rainwater so that it may be purified of its impurities. (Note: I took **Salzburg vitriol**, dissolved it in distilled water, and let the solution evaporate in a wide glass flask **until a skin formed** <note>original: *ad cuticulam*; the point of saturation where a thin film or "skin" appears on the surface of the liquid, signaling that crystals are about to form</note>, and thereafter let it flow in the cellar

**Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> and **Rehe** <note>Jodocus von Rehe, a 17th-century chemist and physician</note> also filter the **solution**, evaporate it **until a skin forms** <note>original: *ad cuticulam*; the point where a thin film appears on the surface, indicating the liquid is saturated and ready to crystallize</note>, and let it crystallize. For it always leaves **residue** (if one dries and **calcines** it), even if it were dissolved and treated in this manner a hundred times.

When the **vitriol** has finally been purified by frequent dissolving—which purification must be done with distilled water and in glass vessels—one places it into a **coated retort** <note>a glass distillation vessel reinforced with a layer of clay or lute to protect it from the intense heat of the furnace</note> and distills it **by degrees** <note>original: *per gradus*; slowly increasing the heat in stages</note>, finally using the strongest fire until everything possible has come over.

Take this out of the **receiver** <term>receiver: the glass vessel that collects the distilled vapors as they condense</term> and **remove the watery parts** <note>original: *dephlegmire*; the process of concentrating a liquid by distilling off the "phlegm" or water</note> in a **water bath** <note>original: *Balneo Mariæ*; a vessel of hot water used to provide gentle, even heat</note> until only the red **oil** <note>original: *Oleum*; here referring to concentrated sulfuric acid</note> remains. ## Observations on this Process.

I stated at the beginning that I dissolved

in succession, which I do not consider to be 
strictly necessary. For as often as one repeats it,
one obtains dregs <term>Feces: the solid residue or sediment left behind</term>, for the Earth of Vitriol <term>Terra Victrioli: the solid, earthy element of the vitriol</term>
finally separates entirely from the mineral 
acid <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing with the description of the acidic components of the vitriol</note>

The red, heavy, and thick oil is almost like **Butter of Antimony** <term>Butyro Antimonii: a thick, oily chemical compound (antimony trichloride) that is highly "hygroscopic," meaning it greedily pulls water from the air</term>, which attracts moisture from the air. Therefore, it happens that when one places it in a **water bath** <term>Balneum: a vessel of warm water used to provide gentle, even heat during a chemical reaction</term>, it attracts moisture from the **bath** (even through the pores of the glass), which slowly unites with the most subtle acid of the oil of vitriol <term>Victriol-Oehl: a historical name for concentrated sulfuric acid</term> and separates from the heavy sulfurous part, remaining above it as a clear **liquid** <term>Liquor: a general term for a fluid substance</term>, which **liquid** one can separate through a small funnel. The remainder, however, which does not turn into a **liquid**, is the **embryonic sulfur of the vitriol of Venus** <note>original: *Sulphur embryonatum Victrioli Veneris*; in alchemy, "Venus" is the code name for copper, so this refers to a sulfurous substance derived from copper sulfate</note>, undoubtedly of a solar quality <note>referring to properties associated with gold</note>. This must be distilled over from a small retort <term>Retorte: a glass vessel with a long, downward-sloping neck used for distilling substances</term>; thus a black earth remains behind, in which a salt is hidden. One must first reverberate <term>reverberiren: to heat a substance in a furnace where the flames are deflected or "reverberated" onto the material to provide intense heat without direct contact</term> this black earth under the muffle <term>Muffel: a protective, D-shaped ceramic chamber in a furnace that shields the material from ash and direct flame</term>; afterwards, one extracts the salt with the acidic **liquid** called the **Spirit of Mercury** <term>Spiritus Mercurii: a refined, volatile essence obtained during the process</term>. The **spirit** is then distilled off, so that the salt remains behind as white. The **spirit**, however, must have been previously rectified <term>rectificiret: purified and strengthened through repeated distillation</term> **by itself** <note>original: *per se*</note>.

### Butler’s Stone.

Mention was made earlier of Butler’s Stone <note>original: "Lapis Butleri"; a famous medicinal preparation attributed to an Irish physician named Butler, popularized by the chemist Van Helmont</note>. Its actual preparation, as Van Helmont <note>original: "Helmontius"; referring to Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneer of pneumatic chemistry and medicine</note> has revealed here and there, is as follows: Take the dead head <term>Caput Mortuum: the useless solid residue left over after a substance has been distilled or heated to exhaustion</term>, or the colcothar of vitriol <term>Calcothar Victrioli: the brownish-red iron oxide left after the distillation of sulfuric acid</term>, crush it small, mix in half as much purified sal ammoniac <term>Salmiac: ammonium chloride, used frequently as a catalyst or flux</term>, and sublimate <term>sublimire: to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again on a cool surface, a method of purification</term> it from there. In this way, the sal ammoniac carries the first essence of Venus <note>original: "primum Ens Veneris"; in alchemy, "Venus" is the code name for copper; the "first essence" refers to a medicinal extract believed to have great healing power</note> upward with it. Mix such sublim-

Its virtue <note>In this context, "virtue" refers to the inherent healing power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</note> is great, both internally and externally.

If one takes a spoonful of olive oil <note>original: "Baum-Oehl"; literally "tree oil," commonly referring to olive oil in historical German texts</note> and places a small piece of this stone into it, and then pours off that oil, one can prepare a large amount of other oil with it. It then becomes a great **medicine** <term>Arzney: a general term for a remedy or pharmaceutical preparation</term> that heals **dropsy** <term>Wassersucht: an old medical term for edema, or the painful swelling of the body caused by fluid retention</term> and other incurable diseases, simply by anointing oneself with it; **Van Helmont** <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern chemistry</note> discusses this in his treatise, and **Butler** treats it in detail.

## Sympathetic Powder <term>Pulvis Sympatheticus: a famous "weapon salve" or powder popularized in the 17th century, believed to heal wounds through "sympathy" by acting on the blood or the weapon that caused the injury rather than just the wound itself</term>.

It is made from **Hungarian Vitriol** <term>Victriolo Hungarico: blue vitriol or copper sulfate, specifically sourced from Hungarian mines which were prized by alchemists for their high quality</term> as follows: After the **vitriol** <note>original: "Victriol"</note> has first been purified, one places it in the sun throughout the entire **Dog Days** <term>Hunds-Tage: the hottest period of summer, typically from late July to late August, when the sun's heat was thought to be most potent for "calcining" or drying minerals</term>; thus, the vitriol breaks down into a white powder.

This powder heals all old and new injuries and wounds, if one either sprinkles some of the powder onto the blood, or lets the powder dissolve in water, soaks the bloody linen in it, and carries it against one's bare skin. For example: if someone has been struck or stabbed, let some of the blood be collected on a clean **small rag** <note>original: "Läpgen"</note>. Let some of the powder dissolve in clean water, wash the wound with it, and bind the wound with a dry, clean cloth. 

Moisten the blood [on the rag] with the **solution** <note>original: "Solution"</note>, and carry that rag against your own bare skin, and it heals. If it is an old injury, wash it out with the **solution** of the powder. Likewise, keep the rags against your warm body, etc. The injury will heal. In cases of **dysentery** <term>rothen Ruhr: literally "the red flux," a severe and often life-threatening intestinal infection marked by bloody stool</term>, I have observed that it was cured when one [took] some of the powder...

### Armenian Salt <note>original: "Sal Armeniacum"; known today as Sal Ammoniac or ammonium chloride</note>.

Sal ammoniac is a salt that was first
discovered in Armenia, which is why it
received the name Armenian.
In places where camels, donkeys, and
mules have stood, the hot earth was mixed
with the urine of these animals as well
as that of the shepherds, and with the earth-salt therein,
so that a mixed salt was created by the heat
of the sun. The Venetians were the first
to observe this. And because they
saw that this salt generated itself from
the salt of the earth and the salt
of urine, they believed that one
could make such a salt everywhere from
sea salt and urine; as indeed
has happened. For if one takes salt,
soot, and urine, boils them together,
dissolves them again with urine, and
boils them once more, one obtains
good sal ammoniac, which is a salt
that one today— <meta>catchword: ge (likely "heute zu Tage" - "nowadays")</meta>

...can hardly do without, both in medicine as well as chemistry and alchemy.

In medicine, its **volatile spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus volatilis"; a solution of ammonia in water</note> has its particular use, which is prepared thus: Take **potash** <term>Pott-Asche: potassium carbonate derived from wood ash</term> and **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac: ammonium chloride</term> in equal parts, mix them well, place the mixture into a **flask** <term>Kolben</term> that has a wide **alembic head** <term>Helm</term>, attach a **receiver** <term>Vorlage</term>, and distill gently. Thus the spirit rises over, and with it, a beautiful white salt settles in the **head** <term>Helm</term>, which has the same virtue as the **spirit** <note>original: "Spiritu"</note>: It resists all acidity of the stomach, and that which is contained in the blood and the fluids of the body. From the **residue** <term>Remanenz: the material left behind in the distillation vessel</term> one extracts the salt; this is a good **fever-reducer** <term>Antifebrile</term>. Taking 20, 30, up to 60 **grains** <term>gran: a traditional unit of weight, approximately 0.06 grams</term> of it is also good for the stomach.

When one **cements** <term>cementiret: a dry metallurgical process where a metal is heated while packed in a powder to alter its properties</term> and melts refined silver with this salt, it becomes quite compact, so that it can be more easily **graded** <term>gradiren: a process of refining or improving the quality and color of a metal</term>.

If one dissolves the **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac</term> with common water and quenches glowing brick fragments in it, the bricks draw themselves full of the **sal ammoniac**. Distill these from a **retort** <term>Retorten</term>, and you will obtain a **spirit** <note>original: "Spiritum"</note>,

Dissolve the *sal ammoniac* <term>Salmiac: ammonium chloride</term> in pure water, distill the water strongly off of it, and it will carry a portion of the *sal ammoniac* over with it. Dissolve the *residue* <term>Remanenz: the material left behind</term> again and distill strongly, so more *sal ammoniac* passes over. Dissolve the *residue* once more: remove the *dregs* <term>Feces: the sediment or solid waste</term> from it, and repeat this work until all the *sal ammoniac* is forced over by the heat. Afterward, distill a part of the water away: distill the remainder strongly again, and so the *sal ammoniac* becomes increasingly *refined* <term>subtilisiret: processed to become more volatile or pure</term>. Finally, distill *by water bath* <note>original: "ex Balneo" (Balneum Mariae)</note> whatever will pass, so that a thick *liquid* remains. Place this in a cellar, so that *crystals* form, which are very easily liquified. These dissolve a subtle **gold-calx** <term>Gold-Kalck: gold reduced to a fine powder through chemical action</term> *radically* <note>original: "radicaliter"; meaning completely or at the root level</note> and turn it into a *tincture*. However, one can make the **gold-calx** with *mercuric sublimate* <term>Mercurio sublimato: corrosive sublimate or mercury(II) chloride</term> and *regulus of antimony* <term>Regulo Antimonii: the metallic form of antimony</term> or bismuth, as taught in the *Chapter on the Sun* <note>original: "Cap. de Sole"; in alchemy, the Sun represents Gold</note>. Gold *prepared* in such a manner, when *highly rectified spirit of wine* <term>Spiritu Vini rectificatissimo: concentrated ethanol</term> is poured over it, yields a magnificent *potable gold* <term>Aurum potabile: a legendary medicinal oil of gold believed to cure all ailments</term>.

## A Solvent for the Dissolution of Gold made from Sal Ammoniac and Nitre with Butter of Antimony <note>original: "Menstruum pro Solutione Solis ex Armeniaco & 🜔. cum But. 🜘 ii."</note>

Take 3 pounds of purified **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac: ammonium chloride</term> and the same amount of purified **nitre** <term>Salpeter: potassium nitrate</term>. Mix these as finely as possible and add 6 pounds of **bole** <term>Bolus: an earthy clay used here to separate the salts and prevent them from melting into a solid mass</term>. Grind them well together. Wall a **tubulated retort** <term>Retortam tubulatam: a distillation vessel with a stoppered opening on top for adding materials</term> into a furnace and **lute** the retort <note>original: "beschlage"; to coat the glass with a protective layer of clay and fiber to withstand high heat</note>. Attach a wide **receiver** <term>Recipient: the vessel that collects the distilled liquid</term> and start a gentle fire beneath it. Using a spoon, add 4 **Loth** <term>Loth: a German unit of weight, approximately 1/2 ounce or 15 grams</term> of the mixture through the tube. Plug the tube and watch the fire carefully so that it does not become too strong; the **spirits** <term>Spiritus: the volatile acidic vapors</term> will come over appearing white.

When the vapors have settled, add another 4 **Loth**, and continue this bit by bit until you have finished the entire mixture. Remove the receiver and pour out the collected **solvent** <term>Menstruum: a liquid used to dissolve solids</term>; **rectify** it <note>original: "rectificire"; to purify a liquid by distilling it again</note> once more.

Afterward, take 1 pound of this **solvent** and add 4 **Loth** of **butter of antimony** <term>Butyri Antimonii: antimony trichloride, a thick, oily, and highly caustic substance</term>. Do this very slowly and carefully, otherwise the mixture will heat up and boil over. When the **butter** has dissolved, add another 4 **Loth** to the **solvent**. Repeat this a third and fourth time, so that in this manner 16 **Loth** are added to every 1 pound. Distill it from a low-necked **flask**,

<meta>catchword: flask,</meta>

The process emphasizes the use of a tubulated retort for the safe, incremental addition of volatile ingredients.</summary>

[...continued from previous page] then you have a <term>Menstruum</term> <note>A solvent or liquid used to dissolve solids.</note> which dissolves gold, makes it volatile, and carries it over the **helm** <note>The top part of a distillation apparatus, also called an alembic head.</note>, so that one can afterwards make all kinds of tinctures from it.

## Concerning Common Salt.

Common **salt** <note>Common table salt: sodium chloride.</note> is of great use in **chemistry** and **alchemy**. It must, however, first be purified by melting, **dissolving**, and **coagulating** <note>In this context, to "coagulate" means to crystallize the salt back into a solid state after it has been dissolved in water.</note>. Afterward, mix it with three parts clay, make balls from them, dry them, and **distill** all the **spirit** <term>Spiritus: the acidic vapors or liquid collected during distillation; here, likely hydrochloric acid</term> out of a **coated retort** <note>A glass distillation vessel protected with a layer of clay to withstand high heat.</note> into a wide **receiver**. This spirit should then be purified from its **phlegm** <term>Phlegmate: the watery, inactive portion of a distilled liquid</term> by **distillation**. If one wishes to have this **spirit** stronger, dissolve 1/4 pound of purified **salt** in 1 pound of this **spirit**. **Distill** the **spirit** from it with a strong fire, and it will carry a portion of the **salt** over with it. **Cohobate** <note>To pour a distilled liquid back onto the solid residue and redistill it to increase its strength.</note> it until all the **salt** has been turned into **spirit**. This is **Paracelsus's Lesser Circulate** <note>original: "Paracelsi Circulatum minus"; a renowned alchemical solvent used for preparing medicines from metals.</note>, with which one can likewise powerfully master the **metals**. However, whoever has prepared the **Circulate** described at the beginning of this work has no need for other **solvents**.

## Concerning the Moon, or Silver.

In the preceding sections, the Sun of the *philosophical* heaven—namely gold—and the planets belonging to it have been discussed; we now turn to the Moon, or fine silver, and its stars.

Silver is a metal which consists of a pure mercury <note>original: "Queck-Silber"</note> and a pure white earth. And although there are many who wish to force a blue *sulfur* <term>Sulphur: In alchemical theory, one of the three primary principles (with Mercury and Salt). Sulfur represents the soul, combustibility, and the "oily" or "coloring" quality of a metal.</term> from silver, they only demonstrate their lack of understanding with their foolish *procedures*. For when silver is properly *refined*, one may *treat* it with *solvents* <term>Menstruis: Plural of menstruum, a liquid used to dissolve or extract substances.</term> or *salts* as one wishes; one will bring forth nothing blue from it. The works that can be usefully undertaken with it must indeed proceed mostly from the same foundation upon which the *solar* <note>Referring to "Sol," the alchemical name for gold.</note> works occur. However, if one wishes to work within it *by the wet path* <term>via humida: A chemical process performed using liquid solutions or acids, as opposed to the "dry path" using intense heat and crucibles.</term>, it requires its own *solvent*, because its *atoms* are *shaped* differently—due to the *sulfuric* earth placed between the *mercury*—than the *solar* atoms, which are entirely *homogeneous*. Without further *discourse*, let us proceed to the work:

<summary>This page introduces the alchemical properties of silver (Luna), defining it as a combination of pure mercury and white earth. The author emphasizes that silver requires different chemical treatments than gold due to its unique atomic structure and rejects common misconceptions about its "sulfur."</summary>
<keywords>Silver, Luna, Mercury, Sulfur, Wet Path, Atoms, Alchemical Principles, Gold, Solvents</keywords>

Take silver that is entirely fine, cupel it <term>cupel: to refine a metal in a shallow, porous cup called a cupel by melting it with lead to remove impurities.</term> with lead, and calcine <term>calcine: to heat a substance to a high temperature to remove volatile parts or to oxidize it, turning it into a powder.</term> it until it is very fine. Dissolve it in rectified *Aqua Fortis* <term>Aqua Fortis: Nitric acid, a powerful solvent used by alchemists to dissolve silver.</term>, and pour into it a quantity of pure water. Hang copper plates into the liquid, and the silver will precipitate <note>When copper is placed in a silver-nitrate solution, the silver "falls" out of the liquid as a solid powder.</note>; wash this silver well to remove the acid, and amalgamate <term>amalgamate: to combine a metal with mercury to form a soft, putty-like mixture.</term> it with 6 parts of running mercury <note>original: "Mercurii currentis" — liquid, metallic mercury.</note>. Put the amalgam into clean leather and squeeze it through so that the excess mercury is removed. Grind the remaining lump together with purified salt so thoroughly that nothing of the amalgam can be seen. Put the salt into a glass retort <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck, used for distilling substances.</term> and distill the mercury away from it, so that the silver remains behind with the salt. Leach the salt away with pure water, and you will obtain a subtle silver calx <term>calx: the fine powder remaining after a metal has been roasted or chemically treated; the "ash" of the metal.</term>. Amalgamate this once more with purified mercury <note>original: "Mercurio purgato"</note>, and again grind the amalgam with purified salt—grind it as finely as possible—distill the mercury from it once more, and afterwards leach out the salt again. Thus the silver remains once more as a subtle calx. Dry this, etc.

Take one ounce of **silver**, half an ounce of the **Regulus of Antimony** <term>Regulus of Antimony: "Regulo Antimonii"; the pure, metallic form of antimony. In alchemy, the "regulus" is the refined heart of a mineral, named for the star Regulus in the constellation Leo.</term>, and amalgamate these with 10 ounces of **quicksilver**. This I write for those who are capable of the work and know how to beat what was washed away back into its own kind, and how to collect the weight—or, in a word, how to **anatomize** <term>anatomize: "anatomiren"; in an alchemical context, this means to chemically dissect or break a substance down into its constituent parts to reveal its internal structure.</term> the **Regulus**.

**Part** <term>parting: "scheide"; the process of separating gold from silver. Historically, this was done using "parting water" (nitric acid), which dissolves silver but leaves gold behind.</term> the silver, and you will find **gold**. Amalgamate this same parted silver again with this Mercury, distill the Mercury slowly from it, and part your silver again; it will yield gold once more.

Such parting, however, is not always necessary; instead, do it this way:

Distill the Mercury halfway off, pour...

...the distilled liquid back again, do this often, and finally all the **silver** will turn into fine **gold**, except for a small amount of **dross** <note>original: "Feces"; the waste or sediment remaining after a chemical process.</note>, which settles at the bottom of the glass like a brown resin and has no metallic flow. This simple <note>original: "schlechte"; in this historical context, the word means modest or straightforward rather than "bad."</note> work proves a great deal. For if one can **invert** the <term>Lunar Atoms: "Atomos Lunares"; in alchemy, the fundamental particles of silver, which is the metal associated with the Moon (Luna).</term> through the **Mercury** so that they become **Solar** <term>Solar: "solarisch"; pertaining to gold, the metal associated with the Sun (Sol).</term> in **essence and weight** <note>original: "re & pondere"</note>, why should one not invert them further through more inversions **by the same agent** <note>original: "per eundem Agens"</note>, and thereby bring them beyond the natural power of gold, while **preserving the nature and quality of the Sun, that is, Gold** <note>original: "salva Solis i. e. Auri natura & qualitate"</note>?

But I am venturing again over the cliffs of the processes:

Thus far it has been told how one should master **silver** through the **dry path** <term>dry path: "via sicca"; an alchemical method using high heat, crucibles, and solid materials rather than liquid solvents.</term> or with **running Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio currente"</note>—that is, **quicksilver**—which is the path of the ancients, who walked it slowly but surely and without danger. But now we also want to step onto the **wet path** <term>wet path: "viam humidam"; a method using liquid acids, corrosive solvents, or "waters." It was often considered faster but more volatile and toxic.</term>, which, though it is shorter, is nevertheless slippery, stinking, and dangerous.

## Section II.

Take the **silver calx** <term>Silver Calx: "Silber-Kalck"; the powdery residue—usually an oxide or chloride—left after a metal has been burned or chemically treated.</term>, which has been calcined with purified salt **by means of mercury** <note>original: "per Mercurium"</note>, grind it with three times as much **mercury sublimate** <note>original: "Mercurii sublimati"; mercuric chloride, a highly toxic and volatile salt used as a reagent.</note>, and distill the mixture; thus a **butter** <term>Butter: "Butyrum"; in alchemy, this refers to a thick, oily substance, typically a metallic chloride, which resembles common butter in consistency.</term> comes over. Weigh what remains, and mix into it again three times as much of the mercury sublimate; distill it, and thus all the silver comes over, while some **dross** <note>original: "Feces"; the solid waste or sediment remaining after a chemical process.</note> remains behind. Also, the **running mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius currens"; liquid quicksilver.</note> revives itself from the sublimate, which is to be set aside.

In this **butter**, the silver is now dissolved by the **spirits of salt and vitriol** <note>original: "Spiritus Salis und Victrioli"; these are hydrochloric and sulfuric acids, respectively.</note>, which otherwise do nothing to silver on their own. But when united with **mercury**, once it has become a sublimate, they attack it, as they do other **metals** <note>original: "Metalla"</note>, and carry it over with them through distillation.

One can indeed **precipitate** <note>original: "præcipitiren"; to cause a solid substance to separate and fall out of a liquid solution.</note> such silver with water; but that should not happen here. Instead, put this subtilized silver into a **vial** <note>original: "Phiole"</note> with a long neck, and **coagulate** and **fix** <note>original: "coagulire und figire"; to turn a liquid into a solid and to make a volatile substance resistant to heat.</note> it; then it **tinges** <note>original: "tingirts"; to "tinge" or "stain" was to transmute a base metal by imparting the qualities of a precious one.</note> tin and lead into silver.

One also extracts the **Sulfur of the Moon** <term>Sulfur of the Moon: "Sulphur Lunæ"; in alchemical theory, "sulfur" represented the combustible or "soul" principle of a metal. Since "Luna" (the Moon) is the symbol for silver, this refers to the supposed essence of silver.</term>, which is, however, a delusion; because

...such a supposed **extract** <note>original: "Extractum"</note> is not a **singular being** <note>original: "Ens singulare"</note>, something **diverse and separate** <note>original: "diversum & separatum quid"</note> from the **entire composite substance** <note>original: "totius substantiæ compositæ"</note>, but rather something dissolved from the whole **substance**, where every little **particle** or **most subtle atom** is nothing other than the **substance** as it was in its full magnitude, according to its essence. The **process** is this: Dissolve silver in **spirit of nitre** <term>Spirit of Nitre: "Spiritu Nitri"; nitric acid, used here to dissolve the silver.</term>, and distill as much of the **solution** so that the remainder can **crystallize**. Mix these **crystals** with twice as much purified **vitriol** <term>Vitriol: "Victriol"; metal sulfates (like iron or copper sulfate) used to produce acidic vapors during distillation.</term> and distill; thus you obtain a **solvent** <term>Solvent: "Menstruum"; a liquid used to dissolve or extract a solid substance.</term> for the **extraction** of the following **prepared** silver.

Take silver **crystals**, which are also called **vitriol of the moon** <note>original: "Vitriolum Lunæ"; silver nitrate.</note>, mix into them three times the weight of purified **sal ammoniac** <term>Sal Ammoniac: "Salmiac"; ammonium chloride, frequently used in alchemical sublimations to help "lift" metals.</term>, and **sublimate**; in this way, the **sal ammoniac** carries a **portion** of the silver up with it. Pour the aforementioned **solvent** over it: let it stand in **digestion** <term>Digestion: "Digestione"; a process of gentle heating to encourage dissolution or chemical change.</term>, and thus the **solvent extracts** the so-called **sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphur"; in alchemy, "sulfur" refers to the flammable or "soul" principle of a metal, not the chemical element alone.</note>. **Sweeten** <term>Sweeten: "edulcorire"; to wash away acids or salts until a substance is neutral, often using alcohol.</term> this with **spirit of wine** <term>Spirit of Wine: "Spiritu Vini"; high-proof alcohol.</term>, which is done by **distilling** the **spirit** from it repeatedly until the **sulfur** remains behind as a sweet **liquid**. Pour this **liquid** onto four times its weight of **animated mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii animati"; quicksilver that has been "awakened" or purified for alchemical use.</note>, and place the **vial** in a **sand bath** <term>Sand Bath: "Sand-Capelle"; a container filled with sand heated from below to provide a steady, even temperature for chemical vessels.</term>...

## Of Tin.

Tin is a metal which consists of **quicksilver** <note>original: "Queck-Silber"</note> and a white, sulfurous, dirty **earth** <term>Earth: "Terra"; the solid, non-metallic residue or "body" of a substance.</term> which makes the **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> quite foul in its course.

Many things are prepared from it: Some **sublime** <term>Sublime: "sublimiren"; the process of heating a solid so it turns into vapor and then solidifies again as a purified powder.</term> it with **nitre** <note>original: "Nitro"; potassium nitrate or saltpeter.</note>, and afterwards produce **running mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium currentem"; liquid metallic mercury.</note> out of the **flowers** <term>Flowers: "Floribus"; the fine powder or "sublimate" collected after heating a substance.</term> using a strong lye. The best **preparation** <note>original: "Præparatio"</note>, however, is to file it small, rub the shavings most finely in an iron mortar, afterwards dissolve it in vinegar, and gently evaporate the vinegar; thus one obtains a **sugar** <note>original: "Saccharum"; likely tin acetate, which has a sweet taste.</note>, or a completely sweet salt. Over this salt, pour the **circulate** <term>Circulate: "Circulat"; a solvent or spirit that has been repeatedly distilled or "circulated" to increase its potency.</term>, **digest** <note>To heat gently over a long period.</note> it for a month, and distill it afterwards...

...afterwards, until everything passes over, the **sulfur** <term>Sulfur: "Sulphur"; the alchemical "soul" or combustible principle of a metal.</term> separates from its **mercury** <term>Mercury: "Mercurio"; the "spirit" or volatile principle.</term>. The **sulfur** has its use in **medicine** <note>original: "Medicina"; this could refer to human medicine or the "medicine" used to heal/transform base metals.</note>. **Calcine** <term>Calcine: "Calcinire"; to roast a metal until it turns into a dry powder or oxide.</term> the tin in fire in this manner: melt it with equal parts of **lead** <note>original: "Bley"</note> in a **refining dish** <term>Refining dish: "Treibe-Scherben"; a shallow clay vessel used for cupellation or separating metals.</term>, so a white **lime** <term>Lime: "Kalck"; a calx or metal oxide powder.</term> rises over the lead. Take this off, place it on a **dish** under the **muffle** <term>Muffle: "Muffel"; a semi-cylindrical ceramic chamber in a furnace that protects the material from direct flame.</term>, heat it until it glows, and stir it with a hook so that it becomes quite fine. Then take 1 **loth** <term>Loth: A pre-metric weight unit, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce.</term> of **copper** <note>original: "Kupfer"</note> and 2 loth of **Regulus of Mars** <term>Regulus of Mars: "Regulum Martis"; pure metallic iron produced through alchemical reduction.</term>, melt them together, crush the **mass** <note>original: "Massam"</note> into small pieces, and rub into it 6 loth of **sublimated mercury** <term>Sublimated mercury: "Mercurii sublimati"; mercury(II) chloride, a highly corrosive salt.</term>. **Distill** from a glass **retort** <term>Retort: "Retorte"; a glass vessel with a long downward neck used for distilling volatile substances.</term> that has a wide neck, bringing over the **butter** <term>Butter: "Butyrum"; a chemical term for a thick, oily, or pasty chloride substance.</term>. Pour this butter onto the **lime of Jupiter** <term>Lime of Jupiter: "Calcem Jovis"; since Jupiter is the alchemical symbol for tin, this refers to the tin oxide prepared earlier.</term>, and **coagulate** it slowly; finally, apply a strong fire and heat it until it glows, and you will obtain a red **lime**.

Smelt this **lime** with lead, and refine the **regulus** <term>Regulus: "König"; literally "king," the button of pure metal that remains at the bottom of the crucible after refining.</term>, and you will find a good portion of **silver**, of which a fifth part is **gold**.

An even more useful **particular process** <term>Particular: "Particulare"; an alchemical procedure intended to produce a specific amount of precious metal, rather than the "universal" transmutation of the Philosopher’s Stone.</term> can be made from **tin** in the following way:

Make a **regulus** <term>Regulus: "Regulum"; the metallic heart or button of a mineral that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting, named for the "Little King."</term> from 1 part **iron** and 2 parts **antimony** <note>original: "Antimonii"</note>; purify it only once with **saltpeter** and **tartar** <note>original: "Weinstein"; potassium bitartrate used as a flux.</note>. Now take one part **copper** and 1 part **tin**; take as much of the **regulus** as these two weigh together. For example: 1 **loth** <term>Loth: A historical unit of weight, roughly half an ounce or 15 grams.</term> of copper, 1 loth of tin, and 4 loth of the **regulus**. Melt them together, crush the **mass** <note>original: "Massam"</note> into small pieces, and grind into it 12 loth of **sublimated mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii sublimati"; likely corrosive sublimate or mercury(II) chloride.</note>. Place this mixture into a coated glass **retort** <term>Retort: A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances through heat.</term> with a wide neck. A **butter** <term>Butter: "Butyrum"; in alchemy, a thick, oily, or wax-like substance, typically a metallic chloride.</term> will pass over, golden in color, weighing about 6 loth. This **butter** is followed by **running mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius currens"; liquid elemental mercury.</note>, about 10 loth.

Pour this **butter** onto 10 loth of **tin-ash** <note>tin oxide</note> and **coagulate** <term>Coagulate: "coagulire"; to thicken or solidify a liquid substance, often through gentle evaporation or chemical reaction.</term> it gently. Finally, increase the fire until it glows. One can perform the coagulation in a small retort and attach a **receiver** <note>original: "Vorlage"; the vessel used to collect distilled vapors.</note>; in this way, one recovers part of the **butter**, from which one can make a **Philosophical Spirit of Vitriol** <note>original: "Spiritum Victrioli Philosophicum"; a potent acidic distillate.</note> and a red **diaphoretic** <term>Diaphoretic: "Diaphoreticum"; a medicinal substance used to induce sweating, often used in historical medicine to "sweat out" a disease.</term>, as will be reported hereafter.

Smelt <note>original: "siede... an"; literally "boil onto," referring here to the process of scorification or smelting.</note> this **tin-ash** on a **refining dish** <term>Refining dish: "Treibe-Scherben"; a shallow clay dish used to separate precious metals from base metals like lead.</term> with sufficient **lead**, and

Let it slag away thoroughly and cupel it <note>original: "treibe es ab"; cupellation is the process of refining gold or silver by melting the ore in a "cupel" to separate precious metals from base metals like lead.</note>; in this way, you will obtain 10 grains <term>Grain: "gran"; a small unit of weight, approximately 0.06 grams.</term> of silver and 4 grains of gold, likely more rather than less. This is, in truth, a **particular process** <term>Particular: "Particulare"; an alchemical procedure intended to produce a specific amount of precious metal, rather than the "universal" transmutation of the Philosopher’s Stone.</term> which provides a man with a plentiful living. For a hundred pounds of the **butter** easily yields fine gold to the value of a thousand **ducats** <term>Ducat: "Ducaten"; a high-purity gold coin used as a standard currency across Europe.</term>, and one can easily produce and process such a hundred pounds in fifty weeks.

## On Saturn or Lead.

**Lead** <note>original: "Saturno"; alchemists used the names of planets for metals. Saturn, being heavy and "slow," was the name for lead.</note> likewise consists of a nasty sulfurous earth and **running quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver: "Queck-Silber"; another name for mercury.</term>, and its decomposition is the same as that of tin. Many believe they can produce gold with less effort from the **Mercury of Saturn** <term>Mercury of Saturn: "Mercurius Saturni"; mercury extracted or "animated" from lead ore, which some alchemists believed was more "mature" for making gold.</term> than from any other, but it is likely for the most part a delusion. For the **Mercury of Saturn** is by no means as pure as the common kind. Yet it is not

It cannot be denied that many wonderful things can be made from **lead**, which have great utility both in **medicine** <term>Medicine: "Medicina"</term> and **alchemy** <term>Alchemy: "Alchymia"</term>, as will be seen from the following **process** <term>Process: "Process"; a set of chemical instructions.</term>. Take lead, whether it be in the **ore** <term>Ore: "Minera"; the raw mineral form before smelting.</term> or already melted. If it is the ore, crush it small and extract with distilled vinegar everything that is possible. But if it is melted, beat it into very thin sheets or granulate it into dust. Pour vinegar over it and place it in **digestion** <term>Digestion: "Digestion"; a process of providing gentle, continuous heat to encourage a substance to dissolve or react.</term>. When the distilled vinegar has become sweet, pour it off and add fresh, as often and as much as needed, until no more vinegar becomes sweet. Then pour all the **solutions** <term>Solutions: "Solutiones"</term> together and distill the **phlegm** <term>Phlegm: "Phlegma"; in distillation, this refers to the tasteless, watery byproduct that lacks active properties.</term> off of it.

The **spirit of vinegar** <term>Spirit of Vinegar: "Spiritus Aceti"; a concentrated, distilled acetic acid.</term> likes to remain with the lead, and together they form a thick, brownish **solution**. Place this in cold water, and **crystals** will form, which are called **Sugar of Lead** <note>original: "Saccharum Saturni"; lead(II) acetate. It has a sweet taste but is highly toxic.</note>. Spread these crystals on a glass plate and cover them with clean paper so that no impurities fall in; let them stand in gentle heat until they crumble into a white powder. Grind this powder very clear with **spirit of wine** <note>original: alchemical symbol for Spiritus Vini; high-proof alcohol or ethanol.</note>, put it in a glass flask, and **dissolve** <note>original: "solvire"</note> it again—

For the Mercury of Lead is by no means as pure as common mercury. However...

and so the **tincture is augmented in both quantity and quality** <note>original: "quantitate & qualitate augmentiret".</note>.

The fixed stone of the first 
order is also made liquid and increased by 
the **mercury of lead** <term>Mercurium Saturni</term>, by adding 
such in place of the red **liquor**: but the 
first method is better.

The **Preparation of Mercury** <term>Præparatio Mercurii</term> is thus: Take

<header>( o )</header>

...of the purest crystals of **lead** <term>Saturnus</term> <note>The alchemists associated the planet Saturn with the metal lead.</note> 1 part, 
purified **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac</term> 2 parts; grind both 
together as finely as possible. Afterward 
take 4 parts of **saltpeter** <term>Sal Petri</term> <note>The symbol 🜕 represents saltpeter (potassium nitrate).</note>; grind it into 
the first mixture with **vinegar** <note>The symbol 🜖 represents Acetum, or vinegar.</note>. NB: rub it as 
diligently as painters are accustomed to grind-
ing their colors. Then place it into a stone **flask** <term>Kolben</term>, 
pour **vinegar** <note>original: 🜖</note> upon it gradually—not too much 
at once, otherwise it will seethe <note>original: "tumultuiret"; referring to a violent chemical reaction or effervescence.</note> and over-
flow. When enough vinegar has been poured on, 
and no more seething is to be feared, 
shake it well together and set it 
for 4 weeks in **digestion**. Afterward, distill 
all moisture from it; finally, give a strong fire, 
and the **living mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> will come over <note>This refers to the mercury being recovered in its liquid metallic form through distillation.</note>. 
With this, the Stone can be made easily flu-
id, as well as increased through 
repeated labor.

In a **particular work** <term>Particulariter</term> <note>A "particular" process is one that targets a specific metal or result, rather than the "Universal" goal of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> nothing more will 
be found in lead than if one ferments its **calx** <term>Calcem</term> <note>A powder produced by heating a metal to its oxidation point.</note> 
with a golden volatile sulfur, 
and solidifies it therein: 
as was taught regarding **tin** <term>Jove</term> <note>The planet Jupiter was the alchemical name for tin.</note> etc.

### On Marcasite or Bismuth.

Bismuth is a mineral which [contains] a 
pure metallic **mercury**, which 
an arsenical **sulfur** <term>Sulphur</term> binds; it 
is a

<page-num>132</page-num>
<header>( o )</header>

...a natural **Regulus** <note>A "regulus" is the metallic heart or purified core of a mineral, often produced by smelting.</note>, and has this unique 
property: that it dissolves into a green color in **nitric acid** <note>original: "Aqua fort"; Aqua fortis.</note>, from which one may conclude that a 
**cupreous sulfur** <note>A sulfurous essence related to copper, suggested by the green color.</note> must be hidden within it at the same 
time. Its **mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> is separated and 
refined just like any other **metallic mercury**, 
and follows the same process <gloss>through sublimation</gloss>.

When one mixes it with **prepared mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio præparato"</note>, 
one obtains a **butter** <term>Butyrum</term> <note>In alchemy, a "butter" is a thick, oily substance, typically a metal chloride.</note>, like that 
produced from the **Regulus of Antimony**, and it can 
perhaps also be coagulated and **fixed** <note>To "fix" a substance is to make it non-volatile so it can withstand the heat of a furnace.</note> into a metallic 
**calx** <term>Calce</term> <note>The powder or oxide left after a metal is burned or dissolved.</note> for certain uses.

If one pours the **solution** made with **nitric acid** 
into a quantity of warm water, a white powder 
falls to the bottom, which, when thoroughly 
rinsed <note>original: "abgesüsset"; to wash away salts or acids.</note>, is called the **Magistery of Marcasite** <term>Magisterium Marcassitæ</term> <note>Bismuth was frequently called "marcasite" in early chemical texts.</note>. **Reverberate** <note>To roast in a reverberatory furnace where the flame is reflected onto the material.</note> this 
under a **muffle** <note>A clay container used to protect materials from direct flame while heating.</note> for 20 days in succession, 
and afterward extract the salt with **distilled vinegar** <term>☩</term> <note>The symbol ☩ likely refers to a sharp solvent or distilled vinegar (Acetum).</note>. **Ferment** 
it with the **Salt of the Moon** <note>original: "Sale Lunæ"; the "Moon" represents Silver.</note>, and it will yield a 
small **tincture**. **Note:** In the process of 
**reverberation**, the Magistery turns red; this 
could be **imbibed** <note>Soaked or saturated.</note> and fixed with the **butter** in 
which the **solar sulfur of Venus and Mars** <note>The "solar" (gold-like) essences of Copper (Venus) and Iron (Mars).</note> 
resides. Afterward, if refined through **cupellation** <note>original: "abtreiben"; a refining process to separate precious metals from lead.</note>, 
it would and must, without any doubt, 
yield silver and gold.

This **butter**, in which the **sulfurs**...

...of **Iron** <note>original: "Martis"</note> and **Copper** <note>original: "Veneris"</note>, pour it over a

## Spelter <note>original: "Speauter"; an early term for zinc.</note>

It is also a type of **marcasite** <term>Marcasit</term> <note>A historical catch-all term for various metallic ores; here it likely refers to zinc ore.</note>, differ-
ing in that it carries a great deal of **lead** 
within it. Melt this in a clean 
crucible, cover it with fat or pitch, 
or pour oil over it so that it does not evapo-
rate, and shake the crucible with tongs. When 
it has flowed for a while, pour it into a pointed 
**casting mold** <note>original: "Gieß-Puckel"; a conical mold used to separate metals of different densities.</note>, and knock a little 
on the sides so that the lead may settle all the bet-
ter. Strike off the **lead-button** <note>original: "Bley-König"; literally "lead-king," the heavy metallic mass that settles at the bottom of the mold.</note>. Melt the 
**spelter** once more, and even more lead will settle, 
and the **spelter** is then well-purified, 
brittle like a **regulus** <term>Regulus</term> <note>The pure metallic heart or "king" of an ore.</note>, and 
beautifully shiny in its fracture. With this puri-
fied **spelter**, one makes a truly beautiful gold-
colored metal from **copper**, as follows: Take 
1 pound of **copper** <term>♀r</term> <note>The symbol for Venus, representing copper.</note>, let it flow well; when it is

...melts strongly, then let 6 **Loth** <note>A historical unit of weight; 6 Loth is approximately 3 ounces or 90 grams.</note> of well-weighed **Spelter** <note>original: "Speauters"; zinc.</note> also flow in a clean small crucible; pour it into the melting **copper**, stir it a little with a piece of wood, and pour it out. This produces a metal more ductile than **brass** <note>original: "Meßing".</note>, and truly gold-colored. If one adds a fourth part of **silver**, it processes to a fineness of 12 **Loth** <note>12-Loth silver refers to an alloy that is 12/16ths (75%) pure silver, often tested on a touchstone.</note> according to the touchstone. But this joy does not belong in the **Philosophical Heaven** <note>An alchemical metaphor for true, permanent transmutation; the author is warning that this gold-like alloy is "sophistic" or a mere imitation.</note>, because it is not permanent and is capable of much deception; it is reported here only for information. **Note** <note>original: "Nota".</note>, in the chapter on **copper** it was taught how the **Sulphur of Venus** <term>Sulphur Veneris</term> <note>The combustible or "soul" principle of copper.</note> is to be separated from its **Mercurial Body** <term>Corpore Mercuriali</term> <note>The metallic, liquid, or "feminine" principle of the metal.</note>; this also happens in the following way, though with a great difference and a different intention.

Take 1 part **copper ash** <note>Oxidized copper, often produced by heating copper in air.</note>, 2 parts of very fine **glass** calcined into a powder, mix both well, melt it in a **glass furnace**, and let it flow for several hours. Afterwards, reach into the pot or crucible with an iron tool and draw out a little glass; if it is truly green, then pour it out. If your **copper** is then truly white, like fine **silver**, the work is finished; but if it still appears reddish, you must melt it with more glass until it attains the proper fine whiteness. This **copper** is

...a veritable white copper, it no longer turns

which cannot be harmful to an honest philosopher <note>The author refers to "philosophers" in the alchemical sense—those seeking the true secrets of nature.</note>.

Regarding sulfur <term>Schwefel</term>, arsenic <term>Arsenic</term>, orpiment <term>Rausch-gelb</term> <note>A yellow arsenic sulfide mineral.</note>,

not wish to admit such things into the Philosophical
Heaven, but would rather banish them to the
hell of the sophists <note>Alchemists often used "sophist" as a derogatory term for those who used deceptive chemical tricks to mimic precious metals rather than achieving true transmutation.</note>.

Concerning nitric acid <term>Aqua fort</term>, however, because it is highly
necessary, here is this little bit: whoever wishes to separate metals well
must have good nitric acid. For this,
purified vitriol <term>Vitriol</term> <note>Sulfates of iron or copper.</note> and purified saltpeter <term>Salpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, the essential oxidizer for making acid.</note> are
absolutely required; some also add alum <term>Alaune</term> to it,
but this is incorrect.
2 parts Vitriol, 1 part saltpeter, and flint-
stones <note>original: "Feuer-Steine"; added to the distillation mix to prevent "bumping" or to help regulate the heat.</note> mixed in, yield a good parting acid <term>Scheid-Wasser</term> <note>Literally "separating water"; another name for nitric acid because it separates silver from gold.</note>.
Cupel-tests <term>Teste</term> <note>Large shallow vessels for refining batches of metal.</note> and cupels <term>Capellen</term> also belong in our
Heaven; for these, one needs pure, clearly
burnt, and very well-leached ash
from soft wood, and calf bones. 3
parts wood ash, 1 part bone ash,
1/2 part levigated clay <term>Thon</term> in powder form; such

...well mixed, and moistened with pure water,
afterwards beaten as is customary. The lining
<term>Kläre</term> <note>The fine surface layer of a cupel, usually made from highly refined bone ash to create a smooth, porous surface for metal separation.</note> should, where it can be had,
be made of burnt fish-bones; otherwise, the
ash of calf-bones must also suffice. Old horse-heads,
burnt out, are also good. The softer
the bones, the better the ash. The horns
which the comb-makers throw away also
serve for this purpose. However, everything
must be well burnt and properly leached
<note>Leaching (ausgelauget) involved washing the ashes with water to remove soluble salts, leaving behind pure calcium phosphate.</note>.
Thus much has been written for the benefit
and delight of the lovers of chemistry and
alchemy concerning the *Philosophical Heaven*,
to which may be added as a conclusion my
short but thorough explanation of the Emerald
Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus <note>original: "Hermetis Trismegisti Tabulam Smaragdinam"; a foundational, cryptic text of Hermeticism believed to contain the secrets of the "prima materia" and its transformation.</note>.
All true and useful chemical works depend
upon a purification, subtilization <note>The process of making a substance more refined, volatile, or "spirit-like."</note>,
and exaltation <note>An alchemical process intended to raise a substance to a higher state of potency or "dignity."</note> of the natural
forces within the elemental bodies, for which
enough instruction has been given in this little
treatise. May the lover of this noble art
use and employ it for his own and his
neighbor's benefit, but above all to the honor
of God,
->Amen!<-

## W.

**Bismuth** is a natural **regulus**. <term>Regulus: the metallic button or mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible during the smelting of ores.</term> 134  
Dissolves in **aqua fortis** with a green color. <note>original: "Aqua fort"; nitric acid. The green color indicates the presence of copper impurities often found in bismuth ores.</note> 134  
To separate its **mercury**. <note>In alchemical theory, "mercury" refers to the fluid, metallic principle or "spirit" of a substance.</note> 134  
White **copper-green**. <note>original: "Kupfer-Grün"; likely referring to a specific preparation of verdigris or a copper acetate.</note> 135
